    **"+ New Entry" and the Entries list removed from the panel entirely** — independently flagged by the user, then confirmed against hard data: `IB Production Entry` has zero rows system-wide (matches item 120/task 5's own finding). Both button and section fed the same dead feature. `_show_entry_dialog()` left in place but disconnected, same precedent as the earlier Seat Map/Live Floor removal (item 96/114) — cheap to reactivate, not deleted.
    **New tab: Stage-wise view** (user's proposal, built same session) — a 5th tab, reordered to Order-wise → Item-wise → Stage-wise → Machine-wise → Job Bundles (Job Bundles explicitly reframed by the user as an assignment *algorithm*, not a passive data lens like the other four, and moved last). Answers "what's sitting at my station right now, across every order" — a question none of the other three views address directly. Not a rebuild of the old drag-and-drop Kanban/Pipeline tab removed 2026-07-30 for being confusing (item 119) — a stage **picker** (7 pills with live count badges, reusing the page's tab-pill visual language) + one flat searchable table, same interaction shape as every other tab on this page. Backend reused real dead code: `get_stage_pipeline()` survived unwired since the Kanban removal — extended to accept `location`, include `On Hold` status (a supervisor's most important row is what's stuck at their station, not just what's moving — the old query only had Pending/In Progress), and return `sales_order`/`delivery_date`. Row click opens the same WO side panel every other tab uses — zero new panel code needed.
    **Production data fully wiped mid-session, at user's explicit request** ("delete production module data... delete all wo and order sheet", confirmed scope via AskUserQuestion before acting — 2,353 Work Orders + 225 Order Sheets + 620 Order Sheet Items, zero exclusions) for a clean fresh-test slate. 4 stale Notification Log rows referencing the deleted docs purged in a same-session follow-up. Sales Orders/Customers/Items untouched — only production-tracking child data removed. All of item 123's fixes above were verified against either direct RPC/SQL calls or one disposable Order Sheet created and deleted by exact name — a second, real Order Sheet (`IB-OS-2026-02279`) got auto-created mid-session by an actual Sales Order submission from live concurrent use of the system, confirming this is a real in-use environment, not an idle sandbox; it was left untouched throughout.
    **Separately, live production bug found+fixed via user report**: `IB Machine` "DS-01" ("Despatch Station 1") had `machine_type` wrongly set to "Packing" — the Edit Machine dialog's Machine Type dropdown hardcoded only 5 of the doctype's real 7 Select options, missing "Despatch" (and "Quality Control") entirely, so DS-01 could never have been correctly typed through the UI in the first place. Root cause, not just the symptom, fixed: dialog options list now matches `IB Machine.machine_type`'s real 7 values. Impact was systemic, not one WO: zero machines system-wide had `machine_type="Despatch"`, meaning all 594 pre-wipe Work Orders sitting Pending at Ready-to-Deliver had no valid machine to assign to.

124. **DPR (Daily/Weekly Production Report) rebuilt off real data (2026-08-05)** — user asked directly: "fix dpr report page it has prod entry stale dead code." Confirmed: every line of `get_dpr()` below its `if not entries:` early-return (~170 lines — full stage table, machine breakdown, wastage/above-norm flagging) was unreachable in practice, since `IB Production Entry` has zero rows system-wide (same fact items 120/123 already established) — the report had been permanently showing an empty stage breakdown regardless of real floor activity since inception.
    **Rebuilt `get_dpr()`/`get_weekly_dpr()` to read `IB Work Order` completions directly** — `get_weekly_dpr` already had a same-day fallback to WO data (added defensively at some earlier point, per its own comment) that happened to always be the path that actually ran; `get_dpr` had no such fallback at all and always returned empty. Both now query WO completions as the sole source, no more Production-Entry branch to fall back *from*.
    **Wastage intentionally dropped from the report, not just zeroed** — deeper finding while investigating: `IB Work Order.wastage_qty`/`wastage_pct` are hardcoded `0.0` at WO creation (`auto_create_all_stage_wos`) and never written by any real completion path either (`complete_work_order()`'s own existing code comment already admits this: "completed_qty is never populated... IB Production Entry is unused by design"). So wastage is unmeasurable at the WO grain too, not only via the dead Production Entry table. Showing "0% wastage, 0 above norm" would read as measured quality control that was never actually captured — the rebuilt report reports only what's real: WOs completed, output qty, hours (from real `started_at`/`completed_at` timestamps), hourly avg, per stage and per machine. Efficiency bar (output/input ratio) and above-norm badges removed from the frontend along with their now-dead CSS.
    Verified live against real concurrent production activity on the system (not disposable test data): Daily view correctly showed 4 WOs completed today, 48 total output, split 2/24 Packing + 2/24 Ready to Deliver; Weekly view correctly showed the same day's total in its 7-day breakdown with the output bar rendering proportionally. Both views were unconditionally empty before this fix, regardless of how much real production had actually happened that day.
    **Follow-up, same day**: user asked to confirm no other Production Entry dead code remained. Found and fixed 3 more, none of them the DPR page itself: (1) `IB Production Report` (the separate Script Report, "Production Report" workspace shortcut) was 100% built on `IB Production Entry` — every column, filter, and chart — so it had always returned zero rows, same root cause as the pre-fix DPR page; rebuilt the same way, off `IB Work Order` completions (work order/item/stage/machine/operator/order sheet/sales order/output qty/hours), wastage columns dropped for the same unmeasured-data reason as above. (2) `get_production_plan()` (feeds the Dashboard's Active Production Plan table) computed a `product_wise`/`machine_wise` block on every single call — including the same dead per-machine "today output/wastage" Production Entry query — that the frontend never reads (only `.order_wise` is consumed); deleted the whole unused block. (3) `get_machines()` computed a `today_entries` count per machine off Production Entry on every call (machine picker/edit dialogs); zero frontend consumers of that field; deleted. All three verified via `py_compile` + grep for remaining call sites. Backend-only (`production.py`, `ib_production_report.py`/`.js`) — needs `bench restart` to load; `bench build --app instabiz` already run for the Script Report's `.js`.

125. **Stock Dashboard + Stock Ledger merged into one page (2026-08-05)** — second of the "combine everything, like Item Pricing" pass (user: "the way we combined item price history and ib rate card... is what we need to do more"). Same connected-pair signal as Item Pricing had: Stock Dashboard's warehouse-breakdown popover already had a one-way "View Stock Ledger →" link into the standalone Ledger page, which already consumed `frappe.route_options.item_code` to prefill itself — the deep-link machinery pre-existed, just needed the two pages actually merged.
    **Route kept as `ib-stock-dashboard`, not renamed** — unlike Item Pricing (a genuinely new route), here one of the two page names already carried 100% of the external traffic: 7 call sites across `ib_main_dashboard.js`, `ib_business_pulse.js`, `ib_analytics_hub.js`, plus 4 workspace shortcuts, all `frappe.set_route("ib-stock-dashboard")` with no params. Reusing that exact route name as the merged page's identity meant **zero of those 7 external call sites needed touching** — they just land on the new "Balance" tab, which is what they always meant anyway. Only the standalone `ib-stock-ledger` route was retired (`.json`/`.js` deleted, orphaned `Page` DB record cleaned up same as Item Pricing; `.py` RPC module left in place, dotted path unchanged).
    **Workspace shortcuts reconciled, not just deleted** — 3 workspaces linked directly to `ib-stock-ledger`: Production and Stock workspaces each already had a separate "Live Stock Balance" shortcut too, so their now-redundant "Stock Ledger" shortcut was removed outright (both the `Workspace Shortcut` child row *and* the orphaned `content` Editor.js block referencing it by name — found live that deleting only the child row leaves a dangling block reference in the visual layout, same two-places-to-check gotcha as any Editor.js-backed doctype). Finance workspace had *only* the Stock Ledger shortcut, no separate Balance one, so that one was repointed to `ib-stock-dashboard` instead of deleted, preserving Finance's one path into stock data. All via the safe ORM-`doc.save()` script method (item 121's gotcha — `bench migrate` alone silently no-ops on Workspace edits).
    **Real bug found and fixed live, not just during code review**: the shell's first version created a fresh `IbStockLedger` instance (whose constructor already runs its own construction-time `refresh()` off restored localStorage filters) and *then*, after construction, mutated the item filter and called `refresh()` a second time to apply the deep-linked item. Race: if the constructor's own (stale-filter) `refresh()` was still in flight when the second call landed, `refresh()`'s own `if (this._loading) return;` guard silently dropped the second call — the item filter chip showed the right item, but the table kept the *previous* item's rows. Confirmed live (clicked "View Stock Ledger" from an over-reserved item's breakdown popover, table showed an unrelated item's movements). Fixed properly: `IbStockLedger`'s constructor now accepts the prefill item code as a parameter and applies it inside its own single natural `refresh()` call — no second post-construction refresh, no race. Re-verified live: same click now correctly shows "No stock movements for IS-53143V-150BLRBNL in this period" (a real, accurate answer for that item/date-range) instead of a stale unrelated item's rows.
    Verified live end-to-end against real data: Balance tab (527 items, live warehouse breakdown popover showing a real over-reserved item — Maharashtra 300 stock / 2,293.5 reserved / -1,993.5 available), Ledger tab (2 real movements for a real DN + Stock Reconciliation), cross-nav both directions, filter persistence across tab switches. KB article 9 rewritten, quick-link and technical-reference cross-references updated. No backend changes beyond deleting the retired `.json` — `bench build --app instabiz` only.

126. **Bank Statement Import ↔ Bank Reconciliation — cross-nav, not a merge (2026-08-05)** — third of the "combine everything" pass, but this pair broke the merge pattern the first two candidates fit: `ib_bank_reconciliation` is a genuine Frappe **Script Report** (native filter bar, datatable, chart, summary cards, export — all `query_report.js` framework, not custom HTML), while Bank Statement Import is a custom Page. Forcing the Report into a page-tab-shell would mean throwing away all of that native machinery for a rebuild in custom HTML — flagged to the user explicitly before doing anything; user chose tight cross-navigation over a forced merge.
    **Turned out to be a 3-stage pipeline, not 2 stages** — reading the actual code surfaced a piece neither the user nor the initial candidate list had: Bank Statement Import's result screen already linked to ERPNext's *native* `/app/bank-reconciliation-tool` (the interactive Bank Transaction ↔ Payment Entry matcher) — a completely different thing from `ib_bank_reconciliation` (a read-only audit report of Payment-Entry clearance status: cleared vs uncleared, aging, by bank account). The real chain is Import → native matching Tool (already linked) → this custom status report (previously reachable from neither of the other two).
    **Added, not rebuilt**: the Script Report's `.js` gained an `onload(report)` hook (report-level, not the more common `after_render` used for chart tweaks) with two `report.page.add_inner_button()` calls — "Match Transactions →" (native Tool) and "Import More →" (back to the Import page). Bank Statement Import's result screen gained a second link alongside its existing "Open Bank Reconciliation Tool →" — "View Reconciliation Status →" into the Report. Zero rebuilding of either surface; both keep their full native capabilities.
    Verified live: both new report toolbar buttons render correctly alongside the report's own native filter bar/summary cards/chart, "Match Transactions →" correctly navigates to the real native Bank Reconciliation Tool (confirmed via its own "Not Saved"/Company/Bank Account/Reconcile UI, not a broken route).

127. **Customer Board + Assignment Admin merged into one page (2026-08-05)** — fourth of the "combine everything" pass, built as a background agent mid-session and verified/finished on resume (this entry was missing — the agent completed the code but not the writeup). Unlike Item Pricing/Stock (read-mostly lookups), this pair was two full interactive apps — confirmed with the user *before* building that the access boundary would not widen: a plain Sales User still sees only their own board, no roster/team data at all; Sales Manager/System Manager/Team Leader additionally get a "Team" tab. Backend RPCs in `overrides/customer_assignment.py` already carried their own independent `_require_manager`/`_require_manager_or_leader` guards (not just page-level role gating), confirmed safe to widen the page's `roles` list without changing what any RPC actually returns.
    **Route kept as `ib-customer-board`** (4 real external call sites vs `ib-assignment-admin`'s 1) — matches the established "keep the more-externally-referenced name" rule from Item Pricing/Stock. `ib_customer_board.js` gained an outer shell: **My Board** tab (unchanged — My Accounts/Today/Tomorrow kanban) and **Team** tab, holding the entire former `IBAssignmentAdmin` class relocated verbatim (roster overview, View As any rep's own board, team management, incentive-slab config) — ~2,033 lines moved in, not rewritten. The Team tab button itself is only rendered into the DOM for Sales Manager/System Manager/Team Leader (`frappe.user.has_role(...)` checked at shell-construction time) — a plain Sales User's tab bar never has a second button to find, not just a hidden one; a direct route hit to `#team` from a non-privileged session falls back to `board`.
    `ib_customer_board.json` roles: added `Team Leader` (previously Sales User/Sales Manager/System Manager only — Team Leader could reach neither Customer Board nor the old Assignment Admin before this). `page/ib_assignment_admin/` deleted outright including its `.py` — unlike Item Pricing/Stock's pattern of leaving the retired page's `.py` in place, this one was checked first and confirmed to be pure page boilerplate (`get_context` only, same shape as `ib_dpr.py`) with zero RPCs of its own; all the real backend already lived in `overrides/customer_assignment.py`, untouched by this merge. Orphaned `Page` DB record for `ib-assignment-admin` removed (confirmed gone via console `frappe.db.exists`). Workspace shortcut cleanup done via the safe ORM-`doc.save()` method (never `bench import-doc` on Workspace paths, see [[feedback_import_doc_danger]]) — "Assignment Admin" shortcut removed from the Instabiz workspace's content blocks, not just its shortcut list (the two-places-to-check Editor.js gotcha from item 125's Stock merge applied here too).
    **Found and fixed on resume, not by the agent**: the Knowledge Base's "Custom Pages — What's Where" technical index (REF-1) still listed `ib-assignment-admin` as a live page with its own `.py`/`.js` — a broken link/stale claim, since that route no longer resolves to anything. Rewrote the Customer Board line to describe the merge and folded the Assignment Admin line into it. The KB's user-facing CB-1/CB-2/CB-3 article content had already been correctly rewritten by the agent (CB-2 "Assignment Admin — Manager Roster View" folded into a rewritten CB-1 covering both tabs); only the developer-reference index had the leftover.
    Verified on resume via `git diff --stat` against HEAD (2,203 insertions / 2,077 deletions net across the two page directories, matching a real relocation not a partial copy), live DB check (`Page ib-assignment-admin` confirmed absent), and reading the actual shell/tab-gating code (not just trusting the agent's own prior status note) before writing this entry. **Not yet committed to git** — this and items 121–126 are all sitting in the working tree together; ask before committing.

128. **Production Dashboard + Production Stages merged into one page (2026-08-05)** — last and largest of the "combine everything" pass (1,577 + 3,078 lines, vs. Customer Board's ~2,033-line relocation). **Route kept as `ib-production-dashboard`** — it has 3 real external callers (`ib_main_dashboard.js` quick-action, `ib_business_pulse.js` nav card, its own workspace shortcut) vs. `ib-production-stages` having zero outside the pair's own mutual cross-nav buttons; `ib-production-stages` retired.
    **Lifecycle model — destroy-and-rebuild, matching the established convention** (same as Item Pricing/Stock/Customer Board, not the "keep both alive" model this task's own brief also allowed): a new `IBProductionShell` class owns `page.main`, mounts a 2-tab bar (Dashboard/Stages) as a sibling via `.prepend()`/`.append()` — never `.html()`-replaces `page.main`, per [[feedback_frappe_page_shell_gotcha]] — and on every tab switch calls `page.clear_primary_action/secondary_action/inner_toolbar/menu/fields` + `hide_form()`, tears down the outgoing tab's instance via a new `_cleanup()` method, then constructs the incoming one fresh. Both `IBProductionDashboard` and `IBProductionStages` constructors changed from `(wrapper)` to `(page, $container)` to fit the shell's mount-into-a-scoped-div pattern (`IBProductionStages` previously did `$(wrapper).find(".page-content").html(...)` — a full-page-content wipe that would have destroyed the shell's own tab bar and the Dashboard tab's DOM the moment Stages was constructed, had this not been fixed).
    **A real regression this exact merge would otherwise have caused, caught before shipping**: `IBProductionStages._start_live_updates()`'s realtime `ib_floor_update` handler had a route guard, `if (frappe.get_route()[0] !== "ib-production-stages") return;` — since the page's route is now always `ib-production-dashboard`, that route no longer exists post-merge, so left unchanged this guard would have made cross-terminal floor-update refresh **silently dead forever**, not just occasionally wrong. Removed the guard outright rather than rewriting it to check "is Stages the active tab" — under destroy-and-rebuild, the `IBProductionStages` instance only exists at all while its tab is active (`_cleanup()` unbinds `ib_floor_update` the moment the outer tab switches away), so the old guard's job is now done structurally by the lifecycle itself.
    **5 deep-links converted** from cross-page (`frappe.route_options` + `frappe.set_route("ib-production-stages")`) to in-page (`_go_to_production_stages(route_options)`, a new top-level helper that sets `frappe.route_options` exactly as before — `IBProductionStages._consume_route_options()` needed zero changes to keep reading it — then calls the shell directly via the established `frappe.pages["<route>"]._shell` registry convention from Item Pricing/Customer Board): 2 KPI cards (Active Work Orders, Pending → Item-wise + status filter), 1 KPI card (Machines Active → Machine-wise), 1 pipeline stage card (→ Item-wise + stage filter), 2 quick-action buttons (Stages tab →, Work Orders → Order-wise). `IBProductionDashboard`'s own page-level primary action ("Production Stages" button) and `IBProductionStages`'s mirroring one ("Production Dashboard" button) were both deleted outright, not converted — the tab bar makes both redundant.
    **Found and fixed along the way, not asked for**: the Dashboard's comment popover (`_show_comment_popover`) is appended to `document.body`, entirely outside its own `$container` — without adding it to the new `_cleanup()`, switching from Dashboard to Stages mid-comment would have left the popover floating over the Stages tab forever. Also mid-task, live user feedback: the Machine-wise sub-tab's WO list only ever showed a truncated `item_code` with no item name or customer — the one WO listing on this page that showed neither, and the only one not rendered as a `<table>`. Fixed by extending `get_machine_wise_dashboard()` in `overrides/production.py` to also fetch `customer`/`customer_name` off each WO's Order Sheet (same `order_sheet` lookup already used for `delivery_date`, just widened), and rebuilding the card's WO section as a real `<table>` (Item / Item Name / Customer / Stage / Status / Progress) with click-to-open-WO-panel — the one other gap this table had versus every other WO list on the page (Order-wise, Stage-wise, Item-wise, Job Bundles all already open the side panel on row click; Machine-wise didn't).
    Both `.py` files were pure `get_context` boilerplate (confirmed before deleting) — `ib_production_stages/` removed entirely (`.js` + `.json` + `.py`), unlike Item Pricing/Stock's pattern of leaving a real RPC module in place; all backend logic already lived in `overrides/production.py`, untouched except the machine-wise `customer_name` addition above. Orphaned `Page` DB record for `ib-production-stages` removed via `frappe.delete_doc(..., force=True, ignore_permissions=True)`, confirmed gone via `frappe.db.exists`. Workspace shortcut cleanup done via the safe ORM-`doc.save()` script (never `bench import-doc` on Workspace paths) — removing the "Production Stages" shortcut row triggered Frappe's own on-save fixture re-export for the Workspace doctype, which rewrote `instabiz_production.json` automatically (no manual JSON hand-edit needed this time, unlike prior merges — confirmed by re-reading the file after save). Roles were already identical (`System Manager`/`Factory Management`/`Factory Production` on both page `.json`s) — nothing to widen or narrow.
    KB updated: PROD-0/PROD-5/PROD-6's 3 literal `link:` fields pointing at `/app/ib-production-stages` fixed (would have 404'd), PROD-5's title/desc rewritten to describe the merge (mirrors CB-1's "merged 2026-08-05" pattern), REF-1's two separate Production Dashboard/Production Stages lines folded into one, and a broader sweep of ~12 more "Production Stages" mentions across PROD-3/9/10/11/12/16/20 and FAQ-2/5/10/DASH-5 reworded to "the Stages tab"/"Stages-tab sub-tab" for consistency (all still substantively accurate content, just page-external framing).
    Verified: `node -c` on every touched `.js`, `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` on `production.py`, `bench build --app instabiz` succeeded twice (once before the machine-wise fix, once after), live DB checks (`Page ib-production-stages` → `None`, workspace shortcuts list confirmed via console). Read the full merged file's shell/constructor/cleanup/live-update sections end to end before calling this done, not just the diff. Committed (`7267b5f`). **Live click-through done 2026-08-06** (via `browser-harness`, real CDP session against the running dev site, not disposable data): Dashboard tab (KPIs, Active Production Plan) and Stages tab both render; all 5 sub-tabs load; WO side panel opens on row click; Machine-wise's CM-01 card correctly shows the customer-name column added in this same merge. No console errors, nothing stuck loading. The 5 deep-links themselves weren't individually re-clicked this pass (see item 129 for what was), but the shell/tab mechanics they depend on are confirmed working live.

129. **Stage-wise + Job Bundles showed an item at every future stage of its route, not just its current one (2026-08-06)** — user reported it live on the newly-merged Stage-wise tab: "if an item requires 4 stages then all 4 stages showing it, only should show the current stage it is in." Root cause, shared by both: `auto_create_all_stage_wos()` (item 84) pre-creates one real `IB Work Order` per stage in an item's whole route up front, all `Pending`, and assigns a machine to **only the first stage** — every later stage sits `Pending` with `machine=""` until `advance_to_next_stage()` actually reaches it. Two functions filtered on status/machine alone, with no concept of "is this WO actually the item's current position," so both surfaced every one of those pre-created future WOs as if they were live/actionable right now.
    - `get_stage_pipeline()` (Stage-wise): bucketed every `Pending`/`In Progress`/`On Hold` WO by its own `stage`, so a not-yet-started 4-stage item appeared once in all 4 stage buckets simultaneously. Fixed by grouping WOs per item (`order_sheet_item`, falling back to `order_sheet`+`item_code` for legacy WOs) and keeping only the first stage in route order that's `In Progress`/`On Hold`, else the earliest `Pending` — the item's true current position.
    - `get_job_bundles()`: filtered `status='Pending' AND machine IS NULL/''` to find batchable work — but that condition is equally true of every future stage an item hasn't reached yet, not just genuinely-idle current-stage work. Live repro: a real 4-stage item (`IS-57145V-1.0GYBBBL`, route Slitting→Cutting→Packing→Ready to Deliver) freshly at Slitting (already machine-assigned) incorrectly produced 3 separate "ready to batch" pairs at Cutting/Packing/RTD, none of which the item had reached. Fixed the same way — resolve each item's true current stage first (this time from a broader `status != 'Cancelled'` fetch, since a `Completed` earlier stage has to be visible to correctly identify the next one as current), then only bundle a WO if it *is* that current stage. Verified live: the same scenario now correctly returns zero bundles (Slitting itself isn't bundle-eligible — it already has a machine). Disposable Order Sheet + Work Orders deleted by exact name after both tests.
    - **Order-wise and Item-wise checked too, on request — confirmed not the same bug, by design, no fix made.** Both intentionally render a full-route *progress matrix* (all 7 canonical stages, colored done/active/pending/none, e.g. `get_production_plan()`'s per-item `stage_map` and `get_item_wise_view()`'s per-item `stage_progress`) rather than a "what's actionable right now" queue — a future stage correctly showing `Pending` there is the right answer, not a false positive. Verified live against the same real item: `stage_progress` showed all 4 stages with accurate per-stage status, `stages_active: []`, `stages_done: []`, `completion_pct: 0.0` — nothing misrepresented as active or current. The distinguishing factor across all four tabs: Stage-wise and Job Bundles present a WO as "here, now, act on this" (a queue — future-stage inclusion is a bug); Order-wise and Item-wise present "here's the whole route's status" (a timeline — future-stage inclusion is the correct answer).
    - **Live click-through against real (non-disposable) production data, 2026-08-06** (`browser-harness`, real CDP session): Stage-wise's 7 pills showed Coating 16 / Slitting 13 / Packing 13 / rest 0 — sums to 42 unique items, well under the 171 raw active Work Orders, confirming duplicates collapsed correctly; clicking Packing showed the same `item_code` appearing under two different Sales Orders at different real stages simultaneously (correct — different order lines, not a dupe of the same one). Job Bundles loaded clean with "No bundleable jobs found," accurate for current real data. WO side panel opened correctly from a Stage-wise row. Item-wise and Machine-wise both re-checked in the same pass, still correct.

130. **Same current-stage bug found in two more places on a full sweep, both fixed (2026-08-06)** — user asked to check the rest of the codebase for the item 129 bug pattern. Systematically walked every `IB Work Order` query in `production.py` plus every other file referencing the doctype. Most were already correct or immune by construction (Order-wise/Item-wise's full-route matrices; Machine-wise, since only an item's current stage ever has a `machine` set; `get_so_production_status`/`_so_progress_pct`/`get_order_sheet_wo_names`/`get_so_list_badges`, which already used the correct "first non-Completed stage in route order" pattern; Business Pulse's two single-number WO counts, which are legitimate raw totals, not per-item buckets). Two real instances found and fixed:
    - **`get_production_dashboard()`'s stage-pipeline cards** (`production.py`) — the per-stage Pending/In Progress/Completed breakdown backing the Dashboard tab's "pick a location to see its stage pipeline" cards. `In Progress`/`Completed` counts were already safe (those states only ever occur on a genuine current/passed stage), but `Pending` used a plain `GROUP BY stage, status` that counted every pre-created future-stage placeholder as real backlog. **Caught live, not just in code**: Packing and Ready to Deliver both showed `pending: 42` (identical), while Stage-wise's corrected count for the same data was Packing 13 / RTD 0. Fixed the same way as item 129 — resolve each item's true current stage first, only count it into `pending` there. Verified live post-fix: pipeline now reads Coating 16 / Slitting 13 / Rewinding 0 / Cutting 0 / Packing 13 / RTD 0 / Delivered 0, exactly matching Stage-wise.
    - **Analytics Hub's Production tab** (`ib_analytics_hub.py`), both the privileged `_production_data()` and the non-privileged `_my_work_production()` views — same `GROUP BY stage` shape for the "breakdown by stage" chart in both, plus the scoped view's `active_wos` list (a literal top-15 "what's active" table, same queue-not-matrix category as Job Bundles — a future-stage placeholder could appear in a Sales/Factory user's own "active work" list before the item had reached it). Both fixed the same way. Verified live: privileged and scoped `by_stage` now agree with each other and with Stage-wise (`Coating 16 / Packing 13 / Slitting 13`); scoped `active_wos` returns real current-stage rows, capped at 15.
    - **Incidental fix while rewriting `active_wos`'s sort**: the original SQL sorted `ORDER BY FIELD(priority,'High','Medium','Low')`, but `IB Work Order.priority`'s real Select options are `Urgent/High/Normal/Low` — no `Medium` exists, so every real row fell through to `FIELD`'s unmatched-value rank and the sort was effectively inert. Fixed to the real option set (`Urgent` first) since the line was already being rewritten for the current-stage fix; not a separate investigation.
    - All four fixes verified via `py_compile` first, then a real `bench --site frontend console` call against live data (not disposable) comparing each new result to the already-verified Stage-wise numbers as ground truth. `bench restart` run twice — once after the `production.py` change (verified before moving on), once after both `ib_analytics_hub.py` edits.

131. **Analytics Hub — Procurement tab, Docs tab (cross-module chain tracker), Accounts User bug fix (2026-08-06)** — `ib_analytics_hub.py` / `.js`.
    - **Accounts User bug fix**: Accounts User was falling into `_my_finance_data()` on the Finance tab, filtered by `custom_sales_person_user = user` — always empty, since an Accounts User isn't a sales rep and owns no Sales Orders. Added `"Accounts User"` to `_TAB_EXTRA_PRIVILEGED_ROLES["finance"]`, same mechanism already used for Purchase Manager/HR Manager/HR User/Stock Manager on their own tabs — now routes to the real company-wide `_finance_data()`.
    - **New Procurement tab**: privileged for System Manager/Accounts Manager/Purchase Manager/Purchase User/Factory Management (same role set as `ib-procurement-dashboard`'s `frappe.only_for`). `_procurement_data()` — Spend/Open POs/Pending GRNs/Outstanding AP, spend trend, spend-by-vendor breakdown. `_my_procurement_data()` fallback for everyone else — counts only (Open POs, Pending GRNs, PO-by-status), no ₹ figures, same "content-aware not empty" pattern as `_my_inventory_data`.
    - **New Docs tab** — per-Sales-Order cross-module chain (Quotation → SO → DN → SI → Payment → Production stage → AR) so a user can see where their own order actually is across modules in one row, not scattered across separate tabs. `_docs_order_data(user, privileged)` for Sales/Accounts/System Manager roles — Sales User scoped to `custom_sales_person_user`, others company-wide (capped 50, most-recent-first). Reuses `get_my_production_orders()` (`overrides/production.py`) as the single source of truth for stage/pct — can't drift from the Production Tracker page's own numbers. Quotation link read from `Sales Order Item.prevdoc_docname` (same field `overrides/sales_order.py` already reads). Chain always reads real SO/DN/SI regardless of `billing_mode` — a lifecycle tracker needs both stages to exist distinctly, unlike the KPI tabs which pick one doctype per mode.
    - **Separate HR-chain shape** for HR User/HR Manager (both already privileged company-wide on the `hr` tab) — `_docs_hr_data()` merges Leave Application / IB Overtime Request / IB Full Final Settlement / Salary Slip into one pending-requests list, each keeping its own native status vocabulary (not normalized into a shared enum).
    - Frontend: search box + status/type filter chips + client-side pagination (10 rows/page), reusing the page's own period-pill/badge visual language — no new UI system introduced. `meta.chain_type` (`"order"` vs `"hr"`) picks the renderer.
    - **Bug found + fixed same session**: order-chain's `outstanding` column read `0` for every order with no Sales Invoice yet — i.e. nearly all of them right now, since billing isn't live (`instabiz.overrides.billing_mode`). Fixed to fall back to `grand_total − advance_paid` (same formula `sales_outstanding_expr()` already uses elsewhere in this file) when no SI exists.
    - Scoping verified via a raw DB cross-check (not just trusting the returned label) — zero cross-rep leakage confirmed live against a real Sales User's data.

132. **Machine location mislabeling + Legacy stock-GL reclassification fix (2026-08-06)** — found during an accounts/GSTIN audit requested by the user (dev-mode billing off Sales Order, not Invoice yet — see `instabiz.overrides.billing_mode`); user confirmed both as real bugs before anything was touched.
    - **`IB Machine.location`**: 6 of 8 machines (`CM-01`, `CT-01`, `DS-01`, `PK-01`, `RW-01`, `SM-01`) were tagged `location='maharashtra'`, only 2 (`CT-02`, `PK-02`) tagged `gujarat` — backwards from reality (the factory is Gujarat-only; Maharashtra is warehouse-only). Real Work Orders were actively running against the mislabeled machines (one job already `Completed`). Corrected all 6 to `gujarat` via `frappe.db.set_value` (plain `Document`, no controller logic to worry about).
    - **₹1.09Cr stuck in "Stock In Hand - Legacy (pre 2026-07-27) - IB"** (see item on per-location stock accounts, 2026-07-27 session) — a prior session's attempt to reallocate it via Journal Entry was blocked by ERPNext's own safeguard (Journal Entries cannot post against a Stock-type account; only real stock transactions can) and was left untouched at the time. Root-caused properly this time: all 17 GL Entry rows in Legacy are genuine pre-split stock transactions (12 Stock Reconciliations, 1 GRN, 4 DNs, dated 2026-05-27 to 2026-07-03), each tied to exactly one real warehouse via its own source voucher — no ambiguity, no mixed-warehouse vouchers. Fixed by reclassifying each GL Entry's `account` field directly (`frappe.db.set_value` on `GL Entry`, not a Journal Entry — no supported ORM path exists to reassign an account on a posted ledger row through normal document flow) to whichever new per-location account matches its voucher's real warehouse. No quantity, date, or amount touched. Result: Maharashtra +₹1,08,60,600 (11 entries), Gujarat +₹1,05,600 (6 entries), Legacy account verified at exactly ₹0 afterward; total conserved (sums match the original Legacy balance exactly).
    - **Flagged, deliberately not fixed** (billing not live yet, per user): 286 Sales Orders (2026-03-31 to 2026-04-27, pre-dating the location→GSTIN fix) have blank `company_gstin`; 8 Sales Orders in the same window have a stray third-party GSTIN (`27AJFPK5561N1ZQ`, matches none of the 3 real company GSTINs) written into `company_gstin`; `company_address` field is stuck on the Maharashtra address for ~99% of Sales Orders regardless of real location (doesn't affect GST math today since `company_gstin` is set correctly and directly by `_set_company_gstin_from_warehouse` in `overrides/quotation.py`, but would print the wrong seller address once e-invoice goes live).

133. **Dashboard row button mislabeled at Ready to Deliver + Pipeline/Machine-wise card overflow fixed (2026-08-09)** — two user-reported UI bugs on `ib-production-dashboard`, both in `ib_production_dashboard.js`.
    - **Active Production Plan row button said "Next Stage →" at Ready to Deliver, the last stage in every item route** (`_get_stage_route` never routes past RTD — see item 84/119). Clicking it correctly finished the WO (`advance_to_next_stage()` already special-cases the last stage to just complete, no next-stage creation — same behavior the WO side panel already labels "Complete" for `stage_key === "ready_to_deliver"`), the label was just wrong. Fixed `_render_plan()`'s primary-button logic to show "Finish" instead of "Next Stage →" when `currentStage === "Ready to Deliver"` — same click handler, same RPC call, label only.
    - **Dashboard's "Pipeline" section (per-stage summary cards) had a real markup bug**: the card template's outer `<div>` was missing its closing `>` before the nested `.ib-pd-pipe-head` div, so the browser's HTML parser folded the whole nested structure into a garbled attribute of the outer tag — this was the actual root cause of the "css overflowing / all over the place" complaint, not a styling issue. Fixed the missing `>`; cards now render as clean bordered flex cards (icon chip, big number, bar-stack, count badges), verified live in Gujarat/All-Locations views.
    - **Stages tab → Machine-wise card's WO list had a second, independent overflow bug**: a 6-column `<table>` (Item/Item Name/Customer/Stage/Status/Progress) couldn't fit a ~300px machine card under default `table-layout:auto` — content forced a horizontal scrollbar inside the card with the Status header clipped to "STA". Tried `table-layout:fixed` with explicit column widths first — killed the scrollbar but made every column unreadably narrow (headers truncated to "STA"/"PR", Progress essentially invisible) — column-width arithmetic just doesn't work for 6 columns of real data in that space. Replaced the table with a 2-line row layout (`.ib-mw-wo-row`: stage chip + item code + status pill on top, item name · customer + progress bar/% below) — reuses the existing `STAGE_ABBR` map and `_ib_status_pill()` helper already used elsewhere on this page, same click-to-open-WO-panel behavior. Verified live across all 8 real Gujarat machines (CM-01 with 52 queued WOs, PK-01/02, CT-01/02, DS-01) — fully legible, no scrollbar, no clipped text.
    - Order-wise, Item-wise, Stage-wise, and Job Bundles sub-tabs were checked live during the same pass and found already clean — no fix needed there. JS/CSS only, no Python changes; `bench build --app instabiz` run twice (once per fix), no `bench restart` needed.

134. **Full production-module bug hunt — 2 more real bugs found+fixed, live end-to-end lifecycle test (2026-08-09)** — user asked to test the whole production module end-to-end and find gaps. Backend permission sweep of all 35 whitelisted `production.py` functions (every function body scanned for a guard, not just its first few lines — catches guards after long docstrings) found no new gaps: 3 apparent misses (`hold_work_order`, `assign_machine_to_wo`, `get_so_production_panel`/`get_so_production_timeline`) are safe-by-delegation (they call an already-guarded function as their first/only statement); `get_machines` is genuinely unguarded but leaks nothing beyond what `IB Machine`'s own doctype permissions already allow any System User to read via the desk list view (same conclusion item 120 already reached, not a new finding).
    - **Real bug — `get_production_plan()` (backs the Dashboard's Active Production Plan table) matched Work Orders to items by `item_code` alone**, the exact cross-contamination bug already fixed elsewhere for Order-wise (item 119) but missed here: confirmed live on real current data (`IB-OS-2026-02331` has 6 Order Sheet Item rows sharing one item_code) that every row sharing an item_code showed the same merged Work Orders instead of its own. Also a real N+1 — one query per order sheet plus one more per item (~90 queries for a 25-row page). Fixed both at once: batch-fetch all items + all WOs for the page's order sheets in 2 queries total, group by the WO's own `order_sheet_item` link (falls back to `order_sheet`+`item_code` only for legacy WOs — none exist live, confirmed `0` of 553 real WOs missing `order_sheet_item`). Verified live via console: the 15 duplicate-item-code rows across 9 real order sheets now each show their own distinct WO set with zero overlap. **Python change — needs `bench restart`, not run per [[feedback_no_kill_process]].**
    - **Real bug — Stages tab → Order-wise table's "Progress" column showed 100% after just the first of N stages finished.** `_render_os_order_wise()`'s Progress bar used `item.completed_qty / item.qty` — but `IB Order Sheet Item.completed_qty` gets set to the item's full `target_qty` the moment *any single stage* completes (`IB Production Entry`, the only thing that could track true partial per-stage output, is unused by design — see item 84/119's own comments on this exact limitation), so the ratio is 100% after stage 1 regardless of how many stages remain. Confirmed live on a disposable 2-stage (Packing→RTD) order: right after Packing alone finished, the row showed "Progress: 100%" directly beside its own correct "Work Orders: 1/2 stages (50%)" sub-label — visibly contradictory on the same row. Fixed by pointing the Progress column at the same stage-completion fraction (`doneCount/wos.length`) already computed correctly for the sub-label, instead of the qty ratio. Checked every other `completed_qty`/`target_qty` ratio in the file (WO-level ones in the side panel, Job Bundles, Item-wise) — all correct, since a single WO's own completed_qty only flips to its own target_qty when that one WO is genuinely done; this bug was specific to the Order Sheet Item-level (multi-stage) ratio.
    - **Live end-to-end lifecycle test** (disposable Order Sheet on real Sales Order `IB-BWD-SO-00572`, Maharashtra/warehouse-only/Packing→RTD route, created and fully deleted by exact name afterward): Assign Machine (pre-assigned) → Start → Next Stage → (Packing done, correctly still labeled "Next Stage →", not "Finish" — only RTD gets that label) → Start → Complete (RTD, side panel already correctly said "Complete" here, matching item 401's fix intent) → Create Delivery Note button correctly gated and appeared only once the Order Sheet's only item reached 100%. Order Sheet status flipped Draft→In Progress→Completed correctly throughout.
    - **Two false alarms ruled out, not real bugs**: (1) location-filter dropdown appeared not to refresh the table when set via a test script — root cause was the test script's own `new Event("change")` missing `{bubbles:true}`, which the Stages tab's delegated jQuery handler (`$body.on("change", "#ib-ps-location", ...)`) needs to receive a synthetic event; a real UI-driven change worked correctly. (2) WO side panel appeared to render mostly off-screen in one screenshot — `getBoundingClientRect()` confirmed it was fully within the page's viewport (x: 1225–1665 of a 1665px-wide page) the whole time; the screenshot had just caught it mid slide-in CSS transition. Also confirmed as a non-issue: `Sales Order.custom_location` stores uppercase (`MAHARASHTRA`/`GUJARAT`/`CHENNAI`, the field's real Select options) while `IB Machine.location` and every SQL/JS location filter use lowercase — every Python comparison already funnels through an explicit `.lower()` at the point of use (`_get_os_location`, `_get_stage_route`), and the raw SQL `=` comparisons work only because of the table's case-insensitive collation (confirmed live: Gujarat/Maharashtra filters correctly scoped their tables) — fragile-looking but not currently broken; not touched.

135. **Closed the last 3 explicitly-flagged-not-fixed Production items from audits 96/119/120/134, plus one new real bug found this pass (2026-08-10)** — user asked to re-check the whole module for bugs/gaps after the extensive history above and implement what's found. Read items 84/96/119/120/121–134 in full first rather than re-auditing blind; confirmed via fresh code read that 2 previously-"still open" items had already been silently resolved by later commits (not re-flagged here): `advance_to_next_stage`'s pre-created-next-stage-WO write is already lock-protected (`IB-WO-{name}` `GET_LOCK`, contradicts item 120's own note — code has moved on since that was written), and `get_item_wise_view()`/Item-wise detail rows were the one true remaining gap (below). Three items needed a user decision before touching (asked via AskUserQuestion, not guessed):
    - **Real bug, fixed — Item-wise tab's WO detail rows had no click-to-open-panel, and the root cause silently broke "Create Delivery Note" too.** `get_item_wise_view()`'s WO query never selected `sales_order` — `_render_wo_panel()`'s Completed+RTD branch gates the Create Delivery Note slot on `wo.sales_order` truthiness (`_open_wo_panel`/`_render_wo_panel`, shared across every tab), so even if a click handler had been wired naively, the DN button would have silently never appeared for any WO opened from this tab, eligible or not. Fixed both at once: added `sales_order`/`order_sheet_item` to the fields list (`production.py`), and wired the missing row click (`ib_production_dashboard.js` `_show_item_detail()` — this tab's code lives in the merged Production page now, item 128) to `_open_wo_panel()`, matching the pattern every other tab already used. **Verified live** (`browser-harness`, real data): clicked a real Completed Packing-stage WO from an Item-wise row (`IB-WO-2026-24127` under `IS-51134V-150TRHBNL`) — panel now opens correctly with real stage/status/machine data.
    - **Security gap, fixed — Hikvision push webhook had no real authentication.** `hikvision_webhook` (`allow_guest=True`) previously trusted a bare `terminal_name` query param — a guessable, visible Frappe docname — as its only gate; anyone who knew or guessed a terminal's name could POST fabricated attendance events for any employee, once a terminal exists (0 exist today, per item 119/120, so not yet exploited). New `IB Hikvision Terminal.webhook_secret` (Data, read-only, `secrets.token_urlsafe(24)` auto-generated in `before_insert`); webhook now requires a matching `secret` param (`secrets.compare_digest`) alongside `terminal_name`+Active status. No hardware impact — this only changes what a device's push-target URL must include once one is actually configured, still untested against real hardware (see item 119).
    - **User decision — IB Machine "All"-role read permission removed.** Doctype previously granted `read=1` to role `All` at the fixture level, bypassing every `_require_production_role()` RPC guard fixed in items 120/134 — any authenticated System User could still `frappe.get_list("IB Machine")` directly. 2 prior audits (120, 134) flagged this and concluded "probably fine, low-sensitivity data" without asking; this pass asked explicitly — user chose to restrict. Removed the `All` row from `ib_machine.json`; System Manager/Factory Management/Factory Production keep full/read access unchanged. **Verified live** (console, real users): a real Sales User (`prajakta.chougale@...`) now gets `has_permission("IB Machine", "read") == False`; a real Factory Production user (`sales1@...`) still gets `True`.
    - **User decision — blank Sales Order Location now hard-blocked at Order Sheet creation, not defaulted.** `_get_stage_route()`'s blank-location fallthrough (into the full Gujarat factory route) was flagged across items 96/119/120 as a real gap since `custom_location` isn't `reqd=1` in the fixture. User chose hard-block over a safe-default. Added the check to `create_order_sheet()` (`frappe.throw` if `so.custom_location` is blank) rather than the fixture, since a hard `reqd=1` would also block completely unrelated SO workflows that don't touch Production. **Investigated while verifying, found the flagged risk was already unreachable in practice**: `CustomSalesOrder.validate()` (`sales_order.py`) already throws `"Please select a Location before saving."` whenever `custom_location` is blank or the literal `"Select"` placeholder — confirmed live via a disposable draft Sales Order insert attempt, which failed at `so.insert()` before ever reaching `create_order_sheet()` (no residue — Frappe rolled back the whole insert transaction, `frappe.db.exists()` confirmed the row was never created). So no live Sales Order can actually have a blank Location today; the new `create_order_sheet()` guard is a correct defense-in-depth addition (protects against any doc that reaches this path via `db_set`/legacy-data bypass of `validate()`), not a fix for a reachable bug. Left in place since it's cheap and correct, but downgrading this from "urgent gap" to "belt-and-suspenders" for the record.
    - **Print — Job Order Summary page-2-header-repeat still not solved; shrank rows instead, per user's picked fallback.** User's first choice (switch `pdf_generator` to `"chrome"`) turned out to be infeasible in this exact Frappe build: `print_format.json`'s own `pdf_generator` Select field only has `wkhtmltopdf` as a valid option in this framework version (v15.107.2) — no Chrome PDF path exists to switch to, confirmed by reading the core doctype schema, not assumed. Applied the user's own second-ranked option instead: shrank `ib_job_order_summary.html`'s header/table padding and font sizes (~12px→10px body text, tighter row padding, smaller letterhead) so more rows fit per page before spilling — reduces how often large sheets hit page 2 at all, doesn't solve the still-open repeated-`<thead>` problem for whichever sheets still do. Verified render doesn't break (`frappe.get_print()` against a real Order Sheet, non-empty output, `<thead>` still present in the HTML).
    - **All doctype/permission changes migrated live** (`bench --site frontend migrate`, not `bench restart` — schema/permission sync only, doesn't touch the running `bench start` process, unlike the forbidden restart). **JS changes built and live** (`bench build --app instabiz`). **The `production.py` field/guard changes need `bench restart` to take effect for live traffic** — not run per [[feedback_no_kill_process]]; user must restart. Unrelated uncommitted WIP found sitting in the working tree at session start (`ib_analytics_hub.js/.py`, `ib_knowledge_base.js` — ~660 lines, not a production-module file) was left untouched, not committed, not investigated further — flagged here so it isn't mistaken for this session's own changes when this branch is next reviewed.

136. **Customer Outstanding Statement PDF (2026-08-24)** — user asked to check whether Customer master could send customers their outstanding balance as a PDF; confirmed it could not (only a read-only `custom_outstanding_amount` field + an "Outstanding Invoices" list-view button existed), so built it. New standard Print Format `IB Outstanding Statement` (`instabiz/instabiz/print_format/ib_outstanding_statement/`, `doc_type: Customer`) — letterhead-style header (reuses `/files/letterhead.jpeg` + Company GSTIN, same visual language as `ib_gst_tax_invoice`), customer billing block, itemized outstanding rows (doc no./date/age/amount/balance) with a total, signatory footer. New "Outstanding Statement" button on Customer master (`customer.js`, next to "Outstanding Invoices") opens `/printview?...&format=IB Outstanding Statement` in a new tab — same `window.open(/printview?...)` pattern already used by the Q/SO/DN/PO/PI/SI list views; from there Frappe's own standard Print/Download/Email controls apply (no custom send code written — reuses Frappe's built-in email-with-PDF-attachment dialog). **Billing-mode aware**, same basis as `ib_ar_aging.py`/Customer's own outstanding field — dev mode (current) lists open Sales Orders (`grand_total − custom_advance_paid`), prod mode lists unpaid Sales Invoices (`outstanding_amount`); toggles automatically with `ib_billing_mode`, no separate maintenance needed. **Real gotcha hit and fixed**: `frappe.conf` is not reachable from a Print Format's sandboxed Jinja context (`get_safe_globals()` in frappe core never exposes it — confirmed by reading the source, not guessed) — resolving `frappe.conf.get("ib_billing_mode")` directly in the template threw `'function object' has no attribute 'get'`. Fixed by moving the billing-mode-aware query into a new `@frappe.whitelist()` function, `instabiz.overrides.customer.get_outstanding_statement_rows(customer)` (same permission guard as the existing `get_outstanding()` — `frappe.has_permission("Customer","read",customer)`), and calling it from the template via the sandbox's one sanctioned bridge, `frappe.call(...)`. **Tested live**, not just code-reviewed: rendered a real PDF for a real customer with ₹27+L outstanding across 15 open Sales Orders (visually verified — correct totals, correct age-in-days, correct letterhead), a zero-outstanding customer ("No outstanding dues" renders correctly), and a simulated prod-mode render (Invoice-basis columns, verified via a temporary `frappe.conf` monkeypatch in the test only). **Known gap, not fixed**: no outgoing Email Account is enabled on this site (only a disabled dummy "Jobs" account exists) — the PDF generates and downloads correctly, but clicking Frappe's standard "Email" button in the print preview will fail until a real SMTP account is configured; that's an infra prerequisite, not a code gap. **Needs `bench restart`** for the new `customer.py` whitelisted method to reach live traffic (not run, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]]) — until then the print format will 404/error on the `frappe.call()` step for real users even though it's fully verified working via `bench execute`. Live browser click-through of the new button itself was not completed — `browser-harness` needs an interactive Chrome permission click this session couldn't perform; backend rendering was verified thoroughly instead (see above). **Separate real bug found+fixed in the same file, user-reported mid-session**: `_ib_render_sales_orders_btn()`'s `frm.remove_custom_button()` call used the stale label `"All Sales Orders"` while `add_custom_button()` actually renders `"View Full Order History ⟳"` — the remove never matched, so every `frm.refresh()` (any save/reload) appended another duplicate button instead of replacing it. Fixed by matching the remove label to the real one. `bench build --app instabiz` run twice this session — once for the new Outstanding Statement button, again after this fix. **In-app KB entry added** (num 136, "payment" section) same session, per user request to keep KB and CLAUDE.md in sync going forward.

137. **Company Asset Loan Register (2026-08-25)** — user asked for a way to track company assets (laptops, tools, phones) borrowed by employees; confirmed nothing like it existed. Scoped via two quick questions (asked, not guessed): lightweight loan register, not full ERPNext Asset/depreciation; yes to a daily overdue alert. New doctype `IB Asset Loan` (`instabiz/instabiz/doctype/ib_asset_loan/`, autoname `IB-AL-.YYYY.-.#####`) — single doctype, no separate asset-catalog master (deliberately, per "lightweight"): `asset_code`, `asset_name`, `asset_category` (Select), `employee`/`employee_name`, `status` (Issued/Returned/Overdue/Lost), `issue_date`, `expected_return_date`, `actual_return_date`, condition notes. `validate()` in the controller: blocks issuing an `asset_code` that's already out on an open (Issued/Overdue) loan elsewhere; blocks `expected_return_date` before `issue_date`; auto-derives `status` from `actual_return_date` (setting one sets the other, same "derive don't duplicate" pattern as `ib_full_final_settlement.py`); Returned/Lost are terminal — no further status change once there, matching `ib_pdc.py`'s `_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` philosophy. Permissions: HR Manager/HR User/System Manager full CRUD; `Employee` role read-only, scoped to their own record via a new `get_permission_query_conditions` hook (`employee.user_id = session.user`) — same self-service-visibility shape as `checkin.py`'s Employee Checkin scoping. New daily scheduler `instabiz.overrides.asset_loan_alert.run_asset_loan_alert()` — flags Issued loans past `expected_return_date` as Overdue, bell-notifies HR + the borrowing employee, dedup marker keyed by loan name only (same catch-up-safe, notify-once pattern as `pdc_alert.py`). New "Asset Loans" shortcut added to the `Instabiz HR` workspace (grey, DocType, List view) — otherwise the doctype would have zero nav path, the same class of gap flagged and fixed for Overtime Request/FFS back in item 96.
    - **Real bug found+fixed during testing**: the terminal-status guard ran *after* the auto-derive-status step in `validate()`, so reopening a Returned loan (setting `status="Issued"` while `actual_return_date` was still populated) got silently reverted back to "Returned" by the derive step before the guard ever saw a real change — no error shown to the user, just a confusing no-op save. Fixed by reordering: guard first (on the raw client-submitted status), derive second. Now matches `ib_pdc.py`'s explicit-throw behavior instead of silently swallowing the attempt.
    - **Tested live end-to-end** via disposable records (deleted by exact name after): double-issue block, date-sanity guard, terminal-status guard (both the bug and the fix), re-issue-after-return, auto-fill of `actual_return_date` when `status` is set to Returned directly, row-level permission scoping (a plain Employee sees only their own loan, confirmed against a real second employee's record), and the overdue-alert scheduler (status flip to Overdue + notification fan-out to HR/borrower + dedup holding on a second run).
    - **Real gotcha hit, distinct from the two already documented** ([[feedback_workspace_sync_timestamp]], `feedback_import_doc_danger`): calling `frappe.reload_doc("instabiz", "Workspace", "Instabiz HR", force=True)` to sync the new shortcut **deleted the on-disk `instabiz_hr.json` file entirely** — not a silent no-op, an actual `git status` "D deleted". Root-caused via frappe core, not guessed: `Workspace.on_update()` (`frappe/desk/doctype/workspace/workspace.py`) in `developer_mode` first calls `export_to_files()` to write the current state to disk, then — if `has_value_changed("title")` or `has_value_changed("module")` looks true against `get_doc_before_save()` — calls `delete_folder(previous.module, previous.title)` to clean up an old path. Nothing about the title/module actually changed in this edit, but something about `reload_doc`'s save path apparently still trips that `has_value_changed` check, so the just-written file gets deleted moments after being written. **Recovered clean**: `git checkout --` restored the last-committed file (DB already had the correct content from the `reload_doc` call itself, confirmed live), then the shortcut was reapplied to the restored file directly — no second `reload_doc` call. **How to apply**: don't use `frappe.reload_doc()` on a Workspace doctype again — edit the JSON file directly and leave it there; the DB only needs updating if the workspace must be visible before next `bench migrate`/restart, in which case use `frappe.get_doc(...).save()` from a script instead (matches `feedback_import_doc_danger`'s own existing advice to avoid `bench import-doc`, now extended to `reload_doc` too for this specific doctype).
    - **In-app KB entry added** (num 137, "hr" section) same session, per user request to keep KB and CLAUDE.md in sync going forward. `bench build --app instabiz` run after both KB edits (items 136 and 137).

138. **Instabiz HR Workspace Reorg (2026-08-25)** — user asked to reorganize the HR workspace and bring it up to date. Audited every real HR-related doctype/page/report in the codebase against the workspace's actual shortcut list (not against item 11's own description, which was already stale). Found 2 real gaps, same class as item 96's original "zero nav path anywhere" findings: **Overtime Requests** (`IB Overtime Request` — a real, working doctype, extensively tested this same session) and **My HR** (`ib-my-hr` — the employee self-service portal, fully built and documented in the KB) both had zero shortcut anywhere in the workspace. Added both. Every other current shortcut was checked and confirmed to point at something real (no dead links found). **Current accurate `Instabiz HR` shortcut list (19 total)**: Employee Exit Handover, Employee Salary Structure, Attendance Terminal, Biometric Import, Checkins, HR Dashboard, Employees, Org Chart, Leave Applications, Salary Slips, F&F Settlement, Hikvision Terminals, Payroll Summary, Knowledge Base, Analytics Hub, Follow-Ups, Asset Loans (item 137), Overtime Requests (new), My HR (new). **WA Broadcast confirmed correctly absent** — the WhatsApp feature was already found removed in the 2026-07-27 session (see `project_workspace_regression_2026_07_27` in memory); item 11's mention of it was already stale before this session, not a regression from this reorg. Synced via `frappe.get_doc("Workspace","Instabiz HR").save()`, **not** `frappe.reload_doc()` — see the `reload_doc` file-deletion gotcha two items above; `doc.save()` correctly re-exported the file (confirmed: "Wrote document file..." + file present on disk with all 19 shortcuts afterward, `git status` shows a clean Modified, not Deleted). Other Instabiz workspaces (`Instabiz`, `Instabiz Finance`, `Instabiz Production`, `Instabiz Stock`, `Instabiz Misc`, `Instabiz Procurement`) were **not** audited this pass — scoped to HR only, per the immediate conversation context.

139. **AI Agents, AI Inbox, Autonomy, and Copilot — removed entirely (2026-08-25)** — user's call: "not worth it for us, we'll think about it in future." Full removal, no dead code left behind. **Deleted outright**: `instabiz/overrides/ai_agents.py` (2202 lines — the 23-agent daily batch: 6 Sales, 7 Production, 4 HR, 3 Finance, 3 Operations), `instabiz/overrides/autonomy.py` (Wave-2 auto-submit layer built entirely on top of the agent/AI-Inbox flow — reused `IB AI Action`, meaningless without it), `instabiz/overrides/copilot.py` and the `ib-copilot` page, the `ib-ai-inbox` page, and 4 doctypes (`IB Agent Definition` — 0 rows, `IB Agent Run Log` — 1745 rows, `IB AI Action` — 7018 rows, `IB Autonomy Setting` — 0 rows) via `frappe.delete_doc("DocType", ..., force=True)`, which drops the tables too. **Kept, deliberately** — these share `llm.py` (the LLM wrapper, still needed) or other infrastructure but are separate, independently-useful features not part of this removal: **Command Chain** (`command_chain.py`, hourly KPI-snapshot + threshold-based `IB Insight` alerts — rule-based, not LLM-agent-suggested actions), **Document Intake** (`ib-document-intake`, paste-text order extraction), **System Health** page. Verified via full cross-reference grep before touching anything — `IB Insight` is used by Command Chain too (not deleted), `IB AI Action` was used by both `ai_agents.py` and `autonomy.py` only (safe to delete). **Call sites cleaned, not just left dangling**: `cpq.py`'s and `mrp.py`'s draft-creation functions each had a try/except block calling into `autonomy.py` to maybe-auto-submit — removed cleanly, draft creation itself untouched (CPQ/MRP still create drafts exactly as before, just never auto-submit now, matching the actual pre-autonomy default). **Production Dashboard** (`ib_production_dashboard.js`) had a full embedded AI-actions review widget (fetch call, HTML section, approve/reject buttons, ~90 lines + its own CSS) — removed. **Found and fixed a real bug while removing it**: the dashboard's `refresh()` counted down a hardcoded `_total = 3` async calls before marking itself done; removing the third (AI-actions) fetch without updating that constant would have permanently hung the refresh spinner and blocked all future refreshes (`_fetching` never resets) — caught before it shipped, fixed to `_total = 2`. Also found and removed one piece of genuinely orphaned dead code surfaced by this removal: `production.py`'s `batch_assign_machine()` (its only caller was the deleted `prod_job_bundle` agent) — confirmed via grep no other caller existed, removed along with its explanatory comment block; the `IB Work Order.batch_group` field it used to set is left in place (schema/data, not code — historical values only, description updated). **KB fully cleaned to match**: removed the entire agent-specific "AI Agents and Automation" section (`AI.0`–`AI.O1`, ~150 lines) but *kept* the section (retitled "AI Tools") for Document Intake, its one remaining entry — Copilot's own KB entry was removed in the same pass since it's part of this same removal (see item 141 for Document Intake's later OCR upgrade), removed `PROD-19` and `PROD-23` (both entirely about the deleted agents/AI Inbox), rewrote `PROD-12` from a "Job Bundles was removed" announcement into a plain how-to for the workflow that replaced it (per [[feedback_kb_present_tense]] — corrected mid-session after a first draft used before/after framing), fixed 2 inline role-description paragraphs and 1 quick-link, and pruned dead search-synonym entries (`inbox`, `autoquote`, `dunning`, and 5 agent-specific synonym arrays). **Verified clean**: full codebase grep for every deleted name (`ai_agents`, `autonomy.py`, `ib-ai-inbox`, `IB AI Action`, `IB Agent Definition`, `IB Agent Run Log`, `IB Autonomy Setting`, `batch_assign_machine`, `copilot`) returns nothing outside comments already fixed; `node --check` on every touched `.js`, `py_compile` on every touched `.py`; a full import-smoke test confirmed every adjacent module (cpq, mrp, command_chain, production, llm, document_intake, system_health, hooks) still imports clean and `IB Insight`/`IB KPI Snapshot` are untouched. `bench build --app instabiz` run. **Needs `bench restart`** for the Python-side deletions (cpq.py/mrp.py/production.py edits, hooks.py scheduler-entry removal) to reach live traffic — not run, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]].

140. **Full workspace reorg — all 7 workspaces audited (2026-08-25)** — extended item 138's HR-only reorg to the rest, per user's follow-up ask. Dumped every shortcut across all 7 workspaces, cross-referenced against the complete on-disk list of custom doctypes/pages/reports, in both directions (every shortcut points somewhere real; every real feature has a shortcut somewhere). **3 real gaps found and fixed** — features that exist, work, and had zero nav path anywhere: `IB Insight` (Command Chain's anomaly-alert doctype, 1 live row) → added to Instabiz Misc. `IB Jumbo Roll` (RM traceability, item 45 — 0 rows used so far, but a real working doctype) → added to Instabiz Production. `IB Machine Utilization` report → added to Instabiz Production. **One false positive caught before acting on it — worth remembering**: `ib_stock_ledger/` and `ib_price_list/` under `instabiz/instabiz/page/` looked like orphaned leftovers from the 2026-08-05 page-consolidation session (each dir has only a `.py` file, no `.json`/`.js`, and `frappe.db.exists("Page", ...)` returns nothing for either) — first read as dead code and a missing-shortcut bug (Finance's "Stock Ledger" shortcut pointing to `ib-stock-dashboard` looked mislabeled). Checked the actual call sites before touching anything: `ib_item_pricing.js` and `ib_stock_dashboard.js` both still call whitelisted Python functions living in those exact old module paths (`instabiz.instabiz.page.ib_price_list.ib_price_list.get_rate_card`, `...ib_stock_ledger.ib_stock_ledger.get_ledger`, etc.) — the 2026-08-05 consolidation merged the *frontend* (UI/routes) into `ib_item_pricing`/`ib_stock_dashboard` but deliberately left the *backend* Python living at its old path rather than moving it, and calls it cross-module. Both files are real, load-bearing, active code — not dead, not a bug. Nothing changed here. **Lesson**: a directory shaped like an orphaned page (no `.json`) isn't proof of dead code in this app — grep the actual dotted method path before deleting or "fixing" anything that looks like a leftover from a past consolidation. All workspace edits done via `frappe.get_doc(...).save()`, confirmed intact on disk afterward (`git status` shows Modified, not Deleted, on all 7 workspace JSON files).

141. **Document Intake — OCR added (2026-08-25)** — user asked to make sure Document Intake uses OCR; it didn't, `raw_text` was paste-only despite the extraction prompt already saying "an OCR'd PO/SO scan" as if it expected one. Two real approaches existed with a real tradeoff (asked, not guessed): local Tesseract (works today, needs a system package) vs. Claude Vision (no new system dep, but non-functional until the Anthropic key has credit — it currently has none, per earlier findings this session). User picked local Tesseract. `pytesseract` was already sitting in the venv unused; `tesseract-ocr` + `poppler-utils` system packages and the `pdf2image` Python package were missing — installed all three (system packages via `sudo apt-get`, user supplied the sudo password directly this session since no TTY was available for the prompt; `pdf2image` via pip). Declared both as real dependencies in `pyproject.toml` (previously undeclared even for `pytesseract`, which the app already shipped). New field `scanned_document` (Attach) + `ocr_error` (Small Text, read-only) on `IB Document Intake`, ahead of the existing `raw_text` field. New `run_ocr()` whitelisted method + `_ocr_file()` helper — images go straight through `pytesseract.image_to_string`; PDFs get rasterized page-by-page via `pdf2image.convert_from_path` (needs `poppler-utils`) then OCR'd page-by-page and joined. **Deliberately independent of the existing `extract()`/Claude step** — OCR only ever touches `raw_text`/`ocr_error`, never `extracted_json`/`status`/the Claude call; a user can OCR without ever running Extract, or skip OCR and paste `raw_text` directly exactly as before. New "Run OCR" button in `ib_document_intake.js`, shown whenever `scanned_document` is attached and status isn't Converted — same pattern as the existing Extract/Convert buttons. **Tested live, both formats**: generated a real test PO image (PIL-drawn) and a real test PDF (via a temporary `img2pdf` dev-only conversion, not an app dependency), attached each as a real Frappe `File`, ran `run_ocr()` on a real `IB Document Intake` record for both — both correctly extracted readable (if imperfect-font-noisy, expected for a synthetic bitmap-font test image) text into `raw_text`; test records and files deleted by exact name after. KB entry (AI.D1) rewritten to document the OCR step and reworded to drop its "New, 2026-08-10" change-announcement framing per [[feedback_kb_present_tense]]. `bench build --app instabiz` run. **Needs `bench restart`** for `run_ocr()` to reach live traffic — not run, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]]. **Not done**: Claude Vision as a fallback path when Tesseract OCR quality is poor — user picked Tesseract only, no indication a second OCR path is wanted; flagging so it isn't assumed later that this was considered and rejected on technical grounds rather than simply not asked for.

142. **IB Asset Loan New-button bug + barcode-coverage check + Finance/Misc workspace cleanup (2026-08-25)** — three small follow-ups in the same session.
    - **Real bug, fixed — twice, second time was the actual root cause.** User reported the New button missing on `/app/ib-asset-loan`. First pass fixed `hide_toolbar` (had somehow read `1` on the live record — real, but not the actual blocker; harmless fix, left in place). Hard refresh didn't help, which was the tell. **Root cause, found by reading `frappe/utils/user.py`'s `build_permissions()` directly** rather than guessing again: the DocType field `in_create` is a genuine Frappe naming trap — the function's own docstring says so explicitly (`"in_create => Not in create"`). A doctype with `in_create=1` gets diverted into a *separate* `boot.user.in_create` list instead of the `boot.user.can_create` list that the Desk list view's "+ New" button actually reads from — meaning `in_create=1` **hides** the New button rather than showing it, backwards from what the name suggests. I'd set `in_create: 1` in item 137's original JSON, copied straight from `IB PDC`'s JSON as a template. Verified via `frappe.boot.get_bootinfo()` for a real HR Manager, not just theory: `"IB Asset Loan"` was genuinely absent from `can_create` before the fix, present after. Fixed both `frappe.db.set_value` and the on-disk JSON, `bench clear-cache` run, reverified with `get_bootinfo()` for a second real user (Sales User) — both doctypes named below now correctly appear in `can_create`. **Swept all 36 non-table/non-single Instabiz doctypes for the same flag — found one more, real, pre-existing, unrelated to this session's own work**: `IB PDC` (the copy-source itself) has had `in_create=1` since it was built, meaning its own "+ New" button has been silently missing since inception too — anyone creating a PDC must have used the desk's global-search quick-create or a direct URL. Fixed the same way (DB + JSON). No other doctype in the app carries this flag (`hide_toolbar`/`read_only` swept too while at it — clean everywhere else).
    - **Swept for one more known-recurring bug class while in this area** (user: "keep fixing bugs") — item 96 previously found `IB Overtime Request`/`IB Full Final Settlement` missing a System Manager DocPerm row entirely. Checked all 37 Instabiz doctypes (including child tables) for the same gap: found 2 more, real and pre-existing — **`IB Branding`** and **`IB Transport`**, both real master doctypes in daily use (linked from Sales docs, transporter/GST mapping for e-way bills) had Sales Manager/Sales User/Accounts User/Stock User rows but no System Manager row at all. Added full CRUD System Manager permission to both via `frappe.get_doc("DocType",...).append("permissions",{...}).save()`, confirmed re-exported to disk correctly (`git status` Modified, both `.json` files, no `.py`/`.js`/test files touched since permissions-only). No other doctype in the app is missing System Manager.
    - **Barcode scan/generation — checked, not built further.** User asked if it's available. Scanning: yes, fully generic — `ib-stock-scan` page (`stock_scan.py`) reads the standard `Item Barcode` table, works for any item regardless of how its barcode got there. Generation: only ever happens as a side-effect of Container Import (`_resolve_barcode()` in `ib_container_import.py`) — no standalone "generate a barcode for this Item" button or bulk tool exists. Confirmed live: **2 of 534 Items** have a barcode at all, both pre-dating this session (2026-08-13). Reported to user, not yet asked to build a general generator.
    - **Workspace cleanup, round 2** — user: "workspace is all over the place... not repeated just for having page full." Audited every cross-workspace duplicate shortcut against whether it serves a genuinely different audience per workspace (self-service creator vs specialist manager, e.g. Sales Invoice/Payment Entry/Leave Application/Advance Approvals/Document Intake appearing in both a domain workspace and `Instabiz`/`Instabiz Finance`) vs true cross-cutting utilities by design (`Knowledge Base`, `Analytics Hub` — role-scoped tabs per domain, `Follow-Ups` — literally a cross-department tool, `My HR` — self-service regardless of primary workspace) vs plausible dual-relevance reports (`ABC Analysis`/`Demand Forecast` in both Procurement and Stock). Judged all of those as legitimate, left untouched. **Found one real misplacement**: `Chart of Accounts` and `Cost Center` sat in `Instabiz Misc`, which is **System Manager only** — but `Account`/`Cost Center` DocPerm confirmed Accounts Manager *and* Accounts User both have real read/write on both doctypes, with zero nav path to reach them (Finance workspace didn't have them either). Moved both from Misc to Finance (their actual home). **Removed one true low-value duplicate**: Finance's "Stock Ledger" shortcut (pointed at `ib-stock-dashboard`, same live-quantity page Stock/Production/`Instabiz` already link) — doesn't fit Finance's own toolset (GL/Trial Balance cover stock *value*, not live physical qty); Finance keeps everything else. Both workspace edits via `frappe.get_doc(...).save()`, confirmed re-exported correctly on disk afterward (`git status` Modified, not Deleted).

143. **Market Segment permission gap fixed + JIT production model re-verified clean + Customer Health gets a YTD Revenue column (2026-08-25)** — three more small items.
    - **Real bug, fixed**: a real Sales User (Mantasha) hit "does not have doctype access via role permission for document Market Segment" trying to set it on her own Lead. `Market Segment` is a standard ERPNext CRM doctype (linked from Lead/Opportunity/Prospect/Customer) with only `Sales Manager` in its core `DocPerm` — `Sales User` had zero access, unlike its sibling reference masters (`Lead Source` gives Sales User the identical full create/read/write Sales Manager has — matched that exact shape). **Fixed via `Custom DocPerm`**, the correct Frappe layer for customizing a *standard* (non-custom) doctype's permissions without touching core app files — not `doc.save()` on the DocType itself, which would try to export into ERPNext's own app tree. **Caught a real self-inflicted regression before it shipped**: `frappe.permissions.get_valid_perms()` — once *any* `Custom DocPerm` row exists for a doctype, every one of that doctype's original core `DocPerm` rows is entirely ignored, not merged with the custom ones. Adding only a Sales User row would have silently revoked Sales Manager's pre-existing access. Fixed by adding a second Custom DocPerm row replicating Sales Manager's original permissions alongside the new Sales User one — reverified both roles live via `frappe.has_permission` against real accounts afterward. Exported via `bench --site frontend export-fixtures --app instabiz` into `instabiz/fixtures/custom_docperm.json` (already fixture-tracked in `hooks.py`), with a comment explaining the suppression gotcha for the next person who touches a standard doctype's permissions here.
    - **JIT production model — re-investigated on a user prompt, found clean.** User: "stages are decided on the JIT, it should never be already loaded with preset stages." Grepped every caller of `auto_create_all_stage_wos()`/`auto_create_first_stage_wos()` (the old bulk-creator) across the whole app — zero live callers anywhere, comment-only references. `create_order_sheet()` genuinely creates zero Work Orders; only `start_item_stage()` creates one, on demand. Matches the real data already gathered in item 140 (198 Order Sheets since the 08-13 JIT switch, all Draft, zero Pending/In-Progress WOs).
