No code changed — reported back as already correct, asked for a specific order if the user has one that contradicts this.
    - **Customer Health — added YTD Revenue.** New `ytd_revenue` column (fiscal-year-start via `erpnext.accounts.utils.get_fiscal_year`, not calendar-year — this is an Indian company, fiscal year is April–March) alongside the existing `mtd_revenue`/`q3_revenue`, same LEFT JOIN shape. Added to both the per-customer row query and the aggregate KPI query (matching how MTD is already both a table column and a summary card), new "YTD Revenue" KPI card + table column in `ib_customer_health.js`, colspan bumped 8→9 for the empty state. Tested live: YTD consistently ≥ MTD per customer as expected, aggregate YTD (₹5.67Cr) sits between aggregate MTD (₹1.09Cr) and total outstanding (₹5.60Cr) sensibly. `bench build --app instabiz` run.
    - **Needs `bench restart`** for the Customer Health backend change to reach live traffic (Market Segment/JIT items are permission/data-only, live immediately) — not run, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]].

145. **Follow-Ups UI/CSS overhaul + real bug fix, Floor Management master, Analytics Hub Docs tab fixes (2026-08-27)** — three pieces, same session.
    - **Follow-Ups real bug**: `get_my_documents()` had no status/docstatus filter at all — for one real rep, 25 of 27 "pending" Quotations were already-won (Ordered/converted to SO) or Cancelled, wasting follow-up effort on decisions already made. Fixed: `FOLLOW_UP_SOURCES` config gained `exclude_statuses` per doctype (Quotation/SO exclude their IB-mapped "Confirmed"/"Cancelled" states; Purchase Order excludes Completed/Cancelled/Closed/Delivered; Leave Application/IB Overtime Request/IB Full Final Settlement exclude their own already-decided states — verified against each doctype's real Select options, not guessed), plus a universal `docstatus != 2` exclusion for all 7 submittable doctypes. Verified live: 27 → 2 actionable Quotations for the same rep.
    - **Follow-Ups UI/UX pass** (2 rounds, second round after live user feedback on round 1): overdue rows sort first (by days overdue), Never-logged next by age; day-count shown ("3 days overdue" / "in 2 days"); phone/WhatsApp icons next to any row with `contact_mobile`; currency formatting on amount fields; page state (doctype) persists across visits. Round 2, after direct feedback that round 1's bootstrap-table look didn't match the rest of the app and that follow-up history was invisible: **rebuilt the whole page to reuse the exact same toolbar/table/badge visual language as Analytics Hub's Docs tab** (`.ib-hub-docs-*`/`.ib-hub-table` component shapes, mirrored locally as `.ib-fu-*`) — explicit filter chips (All/Pending/Overdue/Followed Up) alongside the KPI cards, not just click-a-card; removed the loud colored-left-border-per-row in favor of a plain status pill matching the rest of the app. **New: follow-up history is now visible** — new whitelisted `get_follow_up_history(reference_doctype, reference_name)` (same "mine" ownership check as `log_follow_up`); the Log Follow-up dialog now shows every past entry (type, outcome, notes, who, when) in a scrollable panel above the new-entry form — the log was being written all along but had no read-back surface anywhere.
    - **Floor Management master** — new. Full design doc at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-floor-management-design.md` (written first, 4 scoping questions asked and answered before any code — reuse the real Warehouse tree vs. a new model, additive vs. replace `Sales Order.custom_location`, floor as reporting tag vs. routing constraint, Gujarat-only vs. all locations). Found first: Gujarat already has 3 real floor-level sub-warehouses (Ground/First/Second Floor - GUJARAT - IB, 2 with live stock) that Production had zero awareness of — all 8 real `IB Machine` records were flat-tagged `location=gujarat` with no floor concept at all. Built: new doctype `IB Production Floor` (Link → Warehouse, must be a leaf under one of the 3 location warehouses — validated, not assumed; `location` auto-derived; 5 Check fields for Coating/Slitting/Rewinding/Cutting/Packing capability; `is_active`). New `IB Machine.floor` field (optional Link → IB Production Floor — blank means unmigrated, keeps today's location-only behavior exactly). `_assign_machine_load_balanced()` in `production.py` now filters out any candidate machine whose floor doesn't have the requested stage checked — machines with no floor set are completely unaffected by this filter (backward compatible by construction, verified live via a rollback-wrapped console test: a Cutting-only-floor machine correctly excluded from Packing assignment and vice versa, unassigned-floor machines unaffected throughout). Edit Machine dialog gained a Floor field (Link, filtered to the selected Location, cleared on Location change). Machine-wise dashboard card shows the floor badge next to location. Workspace shortcut added (Instabiz Production → Floors, next to Machines) via the safe `doc.save()` method, never `reload_doc`/`import-doc` on Workspace docs. **Deliberately not built this pass** (per the design doc's own scope): no Floor filter on the Production Stages sub-tabs (Order-wise/Item-wise/Stage-wise/Job Bundles) — the core doctype+field+assignment-logic is shipped and verified, but wiring a 5th filter dimension across an already very large, historically fragile file (`ib_production_dashboard.js`) was deferred rather than rushed; flagged here, not silently dropped. The 3 existing floors' `allowed_stages` and the 8 existing machines' `floor` assignment are intentionally left blank — that data entry is the master's own configuration step, not something to guess from outside.
    - **Analytics Hub Docs tab fixes** (user-reported: "not fully functional, deep links broken, missing ui/ux"). Real bug: `_docs_order_data()` only ever returned aggregate DN/SI *status* per order, never an actual document name — so the "Dispatched"/"Invoiced" chain badges were styled exactly like the page's own `.ib-hub-chain-link` (cursor pointer, hover underline) but did nothing when clicked, a textbook dead-link look. Fixed: DN/SI queries now also resolve the most-recent real document name per SO (verified live against a real dispatched order); Quoted/Dispatched/Invoiced badges are now real links to the Quotation/Delivery Note/Sales Invoice form when one exists — a badge with nothing behind it yet (Not Created/Not Dispatched) stays plain, undecorated text so it's never mistaken for a broken link. Also fixed a real correctness regression introduced while restructuring that query (SI outstanding/invoiced aggregation moved from SQL `SUM` into Python — caught and fixed a duplicate dict key from a copy-paste before it shipped). **Missing filtering**: added a Sales Person filter to the order-chain toolbar for privileged (company-wide) viewers — previously no way to narrow the Docs tab to one rep's orders at all.
    - Follow-up history request ("there should be a way to see what was logged before") was about the Follow-Ups page specifically, not the Docs tab — addressed above.
    - All JS/CSS built (`bench build --app instabiz`, 4 times across the session, each verified clean). `bench --site frontend migrate` run once for the new `IB Production Floor` doctype + `IB Machine.floor` field (clean, both confirmed live). **`follow_ups.py`, `ib_analytics_hub.py`, and `production.py` all need `bench restart`** to reach live traffic — not run this session, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]] (user runs `bench start` directly; never restart the bench process without being asked).

Two new Custom Fields on `Customer`, both plain `Data`: `custom_exim_code` ("EXIM Code") and `custom_vat_no` ("VAT No"), placed in the existing `taxation_section` right after `tax_id` (PAN) and before `column_break_21`/`tax_category` — same section, same visual grouping as the other tax-identifier fields. Added via `frappe.get_doc({"doctype":"Custom Field",...}).insert()` (the standard, additive, safe way to extend a doctype instabiz doesn't own) and exported into `instabiz/fixtures/custom_field.json` via `bench export-fixtures`. Verified nothing broken: `frappe.get_meta("Customer")` still loads clean, field order confirmed correct via live meta inspection, and the fixture export's large diff (208 lines) is entirely `modified`-timestamp churn across pre-existing fields (Frappe's own re-export housekeeping) — zero actual field content changed or removed, checked line-by-line before trusting it.
    - **Also this session: 3 parallel background agents spawned for a race-condition + bug sweep across Sales/CRM, HR/Payroll, and Stock/Procurement (user: "spawn agents for work management") — all 3 hit a shared session-capacity limit mid-task (resets 3:40am IST) and stopped with partial/no reliable progress.** One left a disposable test file (`instabiz/_tmp_racecheck.py`, a `customer_assignment.py` `auto_assign_for_user` repro) which was cleaned up; none are confirmed to have left real code changes — re-verify `git status`/`git diff` for `customer.py`, `customer_assignment.py`, and anything under HR/Stock before assuming that sweep is done. **Not resumed or re-launched this pass** — flagging as genuinely incomplete work, not silently dropped.

147. **Bulk Start Production + Delivery Note Qty-Adjustment note (2026-08-30)** — two asks, same session.
    - **Delivery Note now shows a production qty adjustment.** Before this, a Work Order's Adjust Qty reconciliation (`pcs_to_make`/`logs_to_make`, item 119) was tracked only inside Production — visible as a badge on the Dashboard's Active Production Plan row, but completely invisible on the Delivery Note built from that order. New `Delivery Note Item.custom_qty_adjustment_note` (Small Text, candidate list-view column) — `custom_make_delivery_note()`'s `dn_item_postprocess` (`sales_order.py`) now resolves the DN row's originating `IB Order Sheet Item` → its Work Orders → compares each stage's `pcs_to_make`/`logs_to_make` against `target_qty`, and if any differ writes a note like *"Qty adjusted in production — Rewinding: 72.0 → 65 PCS"* (same "from → to" language the Dashboard's own adj badge already uses). **Informational only — the DN's actual delivered `qty` is never changed**, a deliberate choice: silently shipping a different quantity than what the Sales Order says based on a factory efficiency-tracking number would be a real business-logic change, not what "mention it" asked for. Verified end-to-end live: temporarily set a real WO's `pcs_to_make` to a different value, confirmed the note appears correctly in the actual mapped DN doc, reverted with zero residue.
    - **Bulk Start Production** — one SKU sold as several line items at different dimensions (a real, common pattern — confirmed live on `IB-SGM-SO-00480`, one item_code appearing 5 times as separate Order Sheet Items) meant clicking Start Production once per row, "so much time clicking through it." A checkbox now sits next to every checkable Start Production row on the Active Production Plan (Dashboard tab); ticking any shows a toolbar "Bulk Start (N)" button. **First version used one shared MultiCheck stage-picker applied identically to every selected item — caught as wrong before shipping** (user: "each item can have different stages right, you prompt user for stage selection jit, as much as item") and confirmed live: a real 62-item multi-order test selection showed genuinely different correct next-stages per item (a Maharashtra warehouse-only item defaulting to Packing, fresh Gujarat items defaulting to Coating, one item already past Coating defaulting to Slitting) — a single shared picker would have force-applied one wrong stage to most of them. Rebuilt as a per-item grid (rows = selected items, columns = the 5 stages), each row independently pre-checked with *that item's own* `next_stage_suggestion`, adjustable per row; a banner warns when selected items aren't all at the same point. New whitelisted `production.py` `bulk_start_item_stages(item_stages, ...)` takes a list of `{order_sheet_item, stages}` (not one shared stage list) — pure orchestration, every `(item, stage)` pair still runs through the existing single-item `start_item_stage()` one at a time, so it inherits that function's own locking/location-validation/machine-assignment exactly as if started individually; no new Work Order write path. The dialog also folds in the one-time packing-details form (Brand/Core/CTN/Shrink Film/No. of Logs/Packing Type/Size) — filled once, applied to every selected item that hasn't captured its own yet (an item that already has its own is left untouched); leaving it blank correctly skips items still missing capture rather than pushing them through silently. **Deliberately not a revival of the old `bulk_wo_action()`** (removed 2026-08-13 alongside the JIT model's introduction) — that function mass-flipped raw pre-created Work Order status directly, from before Work Orders were created on-demand; this one never touches a Work Order except through `start_item_stage`, the same single JIT entry point everything else already goes through. Verified live end-to-end on real production data twice: a real 4-item same-SKU batch on `IB-SGM-SO-00480` correctly started all 4 on Slitting with packing details saved (4 real Work Orders created, correct individual qty each); the 62-item mixed-SKU/mixed-stage test correctly rendered per-item defaults but was **not submitted** (grabbed real items across unrelated orders indiscriminately as a side effect of a "select all" test click, not a real business action) — closed without starting anything, selection cleared.
    - **KB updated** (PROD-32, "Bulk Start Production — Several Lines at Once").
    - **Needs `bench restart`**: `production.py` (`bulk_start_item_stages`) and `sales_order.py` (`_dn_qty_adjustment_note`) — not run this session, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]]; both may already be live via the web process's own autoreload (confirmed working live in-browser without an explicit restart), but a full restart hasn't been done to guarantee worker/scheduler processes see them too.

148. **Production Plan filter redesign, live-pulse SVG, full DPR stress test + unit-blend fix, wastage capture, native-report patches, global report PDF export (2026-08-30)** — several asks in one session, all verified live.
    - **Animated live-pulse indicator** — order cards and item-stage labels on the Active Production Plan now show a small pulsing ring+dot (CSS `@keyframes`, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected) whenever that order/item is genuinely `In Progress` right now. `_live_pulse_svg()` in `ib_production_dashboard.js`.
    - **Filter bar redesign** — Active Production Plan's Priority/Stage filters rebuilt as real native `frappe.ui.form.make_control` Select controls (same pattern `list_utils.js` already uses for list-view filter rows), replacing bespoke `<select>` markup. Removed the "Any ETD" risk filter and the "Hide completed items" toggle entirely (hide-completed is now permanently on, no UI control) per user request.
    - **Production module wiped to 0 at user's request**, then re-populated by starting real production on 7 real, already-existing Sales Orders spanning every real case: Gujarat full 5-stage route, 1-stage (AEROSOL), 3-stage with qty extremes (20 → 72,000), 4-stage with an On Hold WO, Maharashtra + Chennai warehouse-only (Chennai's first-ever Order Sheet), and a real bulk-start across 7 dimension-variant lines of one SKU. No fake Sales Orders created — avoids polluting dev-mode billing (`ib_billing_mode` treats SO `grand_total` as revenue).
    - **Real bug found — DPR/report output blended incompatible UOMs.** `get_dpr()`/`get_weekly_dpr()`/`IB Production Report` summed `completed_qty` across rows regardless of `target_uom` — confirmed live: "Coating: 300" was actually 288 PCS + 12 ROLL added together. Fixed across all three to always split output by UOM (`output_by_uom`/per-stage `output` list of `{uom, qty, hourly_avg}`), never a single blended number; DPR page (Daily+Weekly) and the Script Report both rebuilt to render per-UOM KPI cards/stacked table cells/multi-dataset chart. Verified live against independent SQL cross-checks (item-by-item stage/machine/hours math all matched) and via the actual pages.
    - **Wastage capture, first real path ever** (user's explicit design choice: prompt on Complete). `complete_work_order()`/`advance_to_next_stage()` gained an optional `actual_qty` param — `wastage_qty = max(target_qty - actual_qty, 0)`, `wastage_pct` computed and persisted (same `apply_workflow`-discards-in-memory-fields fix pattern this file already used for `completed_at`/`completed_qty`). Frontend: new shared `_prompt_actual_output(wo, onConfirm)` dialog (top-level function, not a class method — both `IBProductionDashboard`'s row buttons and `IBProductionStages`'s WO panel need it) asks "Actual Output" defaulting to target before every completion. Found+fixed a real cross-class bug before shipping: first version put the dialog method on the wrong class, throwing `_prompt_actual_output is not a function` from the Dashboard tab's row button. Verified live end-to-end via a real click: 1440 target → 1200 actual → wastage 240/16.67% persisted correctly.
    - **New `instabiz.overrides.report_patches`** — a safe, additive pattern for customizing a NATIVE (core Frappe/ERPNext) Script Report's client behavior without ever editing the shipped `.js`/`.py`: overrides `frappe.desk.query_report.get_script`/`.run` (via `hooks.override_whitelisted_methods`, same mechanism `get_desktop_page` already used) to call the original first, then chain a small patch on top. Used to: hide Customer Ledger Summary's Customer Group column (`get_datatable_options` chain), and add a real "Handled By" column sourced from `Customer.custom_sales_person` (one bulk-fetch keyed off the report's own `party` rows, no join chain needed since this app already stores it directly on Customer).
    - **Global report row-select → branded PDF export** (user: "not some generic default bullshit" — explicitly rejected Frappe's own plain report PDF dump). New `instabiz/public/js/report_export.js` (global via `app_include_js`) monkey-patches `frappe.views.QueryReport.prototype.get_report_settings`/`render_datatable` (chains originals, never replaces) to add a checkbox column + an "Export Selected (PDF)" button to **every** Script Report, native or custom, with zero per-report setup. New `instabiz.overrides.report_export.export_selected_rows_pdf` renders one shared, deliberately-designed HTML template (wide company letterhead, title, applied-filters line, a real formatted table with a numeric totals row, page-numbered footer) via `frappe.utils.pdf.get_pdf()` — built once, reused by every report, since most reports here (aggregates) have no single underlying document a doctype print format could bind to. Button recolored to the app's own `--ib-primary` accent via a scoped class, not a retheme of every primary button. **Three real bugs found and fixed during verification, not assumed away**: (1) `formatdate()` was used for a datetime+time format string — Babel's date-only formatter strips to a bare `date` with no `.hour`, threw `AttributeError`; switched to `format_datetime()`. (2) Export was reading `report.columns` (the report's full, unfiltered column list) instead of what's actually on screen — a column hidden via the `report_patches.py` chain (Customer Group) leaked back into the exported PDF anyway; fixed to read `report.datatable.options.columns` (the real post-filter/post-render source of truth), also filtering out synthetic UI columns (checkbox/serial, id starting with `_`). (3) The export button vanished after navigating to a second report in the same session — `frappe.query_report` is one long-lived instance reused across report navigations, but Frappe rebuilds the toolbar DOM on every report load, so the button reference went stale (detached from DOM) while the JS-side "already added" flag stayed true; fixed with a `document.body.contains()` staleness check before reusing it. Verified live end-to-end across two different reports (Customer Ledger Summary → IB Party Outstanding Summary → back), including a real mouse-driven click producing a correct PDF (synthetic `.click()` calls get silently popup-blocked by Chrome — real trusted clicks don't).
    - **Needs `bench restart`**: `production.py` (wastage params), `report_export.py`, `report_patches.py` — not run this session, per [[feedback_no_kill_process]]; all verified live via web-process autoreload + `bench clear-cache` (for the two new `hooks.override_whitelisted_methods` entries), but worker/scheduler processes haven't been confirmed to see them via a full restart.

149. **IB Container Import — dropped VAT/EXIM fields, list "Print Label"/"Show Labels" action, pre-submit confirmation (2026-08-30)** — three asks, same session.
    - **Removed `vat_no`/`exim_code`** (and the now-empty "Compliance" section) from the doctype — confirmed gone from the form/meta; DB columns left as harmless orphaned data (standard Frappe behavior, never force-dropped).
    - **New list-view row action**, state-dependent: Draft shows **"Print Label"** — picks the item first if the container has more than one (user's explicit call after I flagged the ambiguity: the existing "IB Container Label" print format is item-level, no container-level fields at all), then prompts "how many labels?" and prints that count independent of the row's actual recorded `no_of_boxes` (nothing's final pre-submit). Submitted shows **"Show Labels"** instead — no picking, no prompt: opens the real `/printview` straight away at the container's actual posted quantities, since those are now final. New `get_container_items()`/`generate_item_labels(container_import, item_code, count)` in `ib_container_import.py`; the latter reuses the existing print format by temporarily overriding the in-memory row's `no_of_boxes` before calling `get_print()` — same pattern `reprint_item_labels()` (already in the file) already used, never touches the saved record. Verified live end-to-end both states, plus a direct 50-label stress test (`pdftotext` spot-checked pages 1/25/50 — correctly "1 / 50", "25 / 50", "50 / 50").
    - **Real bug found via my own testing, not shipped** — submitting via `bench execute` with no request-level rollback wrapper (unlike a real browser submit, which Frappe's own request handler fully rolls back on any `on_submit` exception) left one disposable container stuck `docstatus=1` with a dangling *unlinked* Draft Stock Entry after a valuation-rate error mid-submit. Confirmed this can't happen to a real user (verified the real submit path completes cleanly, `stock_entry` correctly linked, once a valid `rate` is set) — flagging only because it's a sharp edge worth remembering for any future `bench execute`-driven submit testing: always wrap in try/except with an explicit `frappe.db.rollback()`, `bench execute` has no automatic one.
    - **Pre-submit confirmation** (user: "add that confirmation" — after I recommended keeping Submit=post-stock-immediately as-is, matching every other transactional doctype in this app, rather than adding a staging step). New `before_submit(frm)` in the form's own `.js` returns a Promise wired to `frappe.confirm()` — "This posts {N} {UOM}, ... into **{warehouse}**. Confirm the quantities are correct before continuing." Output kept split by UOM, never blended into one number (same reasoning as item 148's DPR fix). Verified live: correct math (150 SQMT for 3×50), correct warehouse, "No" cleanly aborts the submit, "Yes" proceeds and posts exactly as before.
    - All disposable test containers (and their real Stock Entries/Bin/GL/SLE effects) fully created, verified, and reversed — zero residue. `bench migrate` + `clear-cache` + `bench build` all run; no `bench restart` needed (client-script + doctype-JSON changes only, both live immediately).

This was already fully designed in an earlier, never-executed session (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-expense-entry-design.md`) — decisions re-confirmed this session: **HR Manager creates and submits directly** (self-approval, same trust level they already have on Full Final Settlement/Salary Slip — reframed from the original spec's Finance-only angle per this session's explicit ask), Unpaid expenses post against a **dedicated new "Expenses Payable - IB" account** (not shared Creditors). Mirrors `IB Credit Note`'s own structure line-for-line where it overlaps (`AccountsController`, `make_gl_entries()`, a `status` field synced from docstatus). Permissions: HR Manager full CRUD+submit+cancel+amend; Accounts User create+write (Draft only); Accounts Manager/System Manager full. Shortcuts added to both Instabiz HR (primary home) and Instabiz Finance (oversight). **Three real bugs found and fixed via live GL testing, not assumed away**: (1) doctype naming was `format:IB-EXP-.YYYY.-.#####` — mixed the dot-token *series* syntax into a `format:`-prefixed autoname string (which expects `{YYYY}`/`{#####}` curly braces instead), so every record's actual name became the literal unresolved string, second insert attempt then hit a primary-key collision. (2) Creating the "Expenses Payable" account with `account_type="Payable"` (as the original design's own field-filter language implied) turned out to be unbuildable — ERPNext core's `GL Entry.check_mandatory()` requires `party_type`/`party` on every GL line against any real Payable/Receivable-typed account, but `paid_to` here is deliberately free text (not a Supplier link, confirmed live: submit threw "Supplier is required against Payable account"); fixed by creating the account with a blank `account_type` instead (still a real Liability-type leaf account, just not one of the two types core treats as party-mandatory) and widening `payable_account`'s link-query filter from `account_type=Payable` to `root_type=Liability` to match. (3) Hit the same `bench execute`-has-no-automatic-rollback trap as item 149's container testing — a mid-submit failure (while still debugging bug #2) left one real submitted GL Entry with no matching credit leg; cleaned up by cancelling the parent doc (which correctly reverses via the now-fixed code path) rather than trying to touch the individual GL Entry directly (blocked — "Individual GL Entry cannot be cancelled. Please cancel related transaction," expected/correct core behavior). Both Paid (→ Mode of Payment's bank/cash account) and Unpaid (→ Expenses Payable, with `against_voucher` set for later reconciliation) cases fully verified live: correct DR/CR, correct cost center per `location` (reuses `LOCATION_COST_CENTER`, same as every other transactional doctype), correct cancel-reversal, zero residue after cleanup. Live form UI also smoke-tested (Paid/Unpaid conditional field toggling, `+ Add IB Expense` button present — no `in_create` flag set, avoiding the item-142 trap).

Confirmed zero residue via a full-app grep.

and all 4 Knowledge Base entries (ABC-1, VS-1, DF-1, CPQ-1). **Verified `compute_oee()`/`get_machine_day_stats()` in `production.py` are still load-bearing** for the live Machine-wise Production Dashboard tab (`get_machine_wise_dashboard()` calls both directly) despite the Machine Utilization report — the thing that originally justified pulling them into their own reusable functions — being gone; kept both intact, only trimmed their docstrings' now-dangling reference to the deleted report, and confirmed live post-removal (`get_machine_wise_dashboard()` still returns all 8 real machines with full OEE fields, zero error). **One real gotcha hit removing the workspace shortcuts**: batching multiple shortcut removals from one workspace into separate individual `doc.save()` calls failed — Frappe's `Workspace._validate_links()` runs on every save and checks the *entire* shortcuts array, so removing shortcut A while shortcut B (not yet removed, same save batch) still points at an already-deleted Report threw `LinkValidationError` on B, not A; fixed by batching all of one workspace's removals into a single load→strip→save cycle instead of one shortcut per save.
